One — Canon So Far
I will keep this as the factual base and not add motives or details you did not give.
Core Role
• One is the major main character within the five-person ensemble.
• He becomes the leader naturally. Nobody appoints him.
• He simply says what he thinks should happen or what he wants to do.
• His plans are usually better than everyone else’s, so people follow him.
• Leadership becomes necessary because the other kids do not function or move efficiently without him directing things.
• He treats leadership like a full-time responsibility and sees the group as family.
• He becomes somewhat controlling, but not excessively.
Early Timeline

Age
Event

10
Two first sees One beat up the older kids bullying his little sister.

Around 12
The five begin forming into a real group during middle school.

13
One begins selling drugs.

Early manga
One is still young and does not yet have a major personal ambition.


Family
• One grew up poor.
• He has no information about his father.
• His mother has been sick for as long as he can remember.
• She is unable to work.
• One knows she is sick.
• He sometimes picks up her medicine and runs errands for her.
• His mother is loving.
• He has one younger sister.
• One and his sister are very close.
• As he becomes involved in crime, he distances himself from her somewhat to keep her away from that world.
• He remains present whenever she needs him.
Personality
• Quiet.
• Intimidating.
• Charismatic without trying.
• Careful and strategic with words.
• Good at reading weaknesses.
• Good at seeing dynamics, causes, and perspectives other people miss.
• Does not chase leadership or attention.
• Fights when he believes it is needed.
Natural Ability
• One can watch something happen, mentally reimagine it, analyze why and how it worked, and copy or improve it.
• He sees multiple perspectives at once.
• This applies to fighting, people, situations, and eventually business/criminal organization.
• At first, he does not fully use this ability outside physical conflict.
Early Flaw
• One solves problems with violence because violence has always worked for him.
• He has not needed to use his intelligence as much as he should.
• Later, a situation forces him to think rather than fight.
• That becomes the point where he realizes his mind is more valuable than simply beating people up.
Entry Into Crime
• One sees older kids selling drugs in an unorganized, careless way.
• He recognizes that they are operating inefficiently.
• He finds a better way to organize and position what they are doing.
• This lets him take customers from others and begin making real money.
• Later, he realizes money can materially change his life and help with real problems.
Early Major Mistake
• Before age 13, One hurts the wrong person.
• That is the first clear proof that solving everything with violence creates problems he cannot punch his way out of.

1. The person he hurts
Who is the “wrong person”?
• A kid he mistakes for an enemy?
• An older gang member?
• Someone connected to a gang?
• An innocent bystander?
• A friend of one of the five?
• A relative of someone dangerous?
• A teacher, coach, or adult?
• Someone who did not deserve it, but was in the wrong place?
2. Consequence of that mistake
What happens because One hurts that person?
• He starts a larger conflict.
• He gets the group targeted.
• He brings danger toward his mother or sister.
• He gets suspended, arrested, or sent somewhere.
• He loses someone’s trust.
• He realizes the person was not actually the problem.
• Something else.
3. One’s speaking style
You said he is calculated with words. When One does speak, which is closest?
• Very few words, but every sentence lands.
• Calm and direct.
• Persuasive without sounding persuasive.
• Quietly threatening.
• Uses logic to make people agree.
• Talks normally with friends but becomes precise under pressure.
• Rarely talks, then suddenly says exactly what needs to be said.
4. His first money realization
What is the first concrete thing money changes for One?
• He can get his mother medicine.
• He buys food or necessities without asking anyone.
• He helps his sister.
• He sees how differently people treat him when he has money.
• He gains independence from adults.
• He realizes money creates influence and loyalty.

2) CORE API ENDPOINTS
Auth / org
POST /auth/login
POST /orgs
POST /locations
Tasks
POST /tasks (manager create)
GET /tasks/today?user_id=
POST /tasks/:id/complete
POST /tasks/:id/evidence
POST /tasks/:id/flag
Voice completion
POST /voice/complete
input: audio file
pipeline:
speech-to-text
intent match → task_instance
validate → mark complete or request proof
Trust & penalties
POST /trust/recalculate
GET /users/:id/trust
POST /penalty/escalate
POST /penalty/clear
Rewards
GET /rewards
POST /rewards/claim
GET /users/:id/rewards
Analytics
GET /dashboard/manager
GET /analytics/location
GET /analytics/user/:id
3) EVENT FLOW (HOW IT ACTUALLY RUNS)
A. Task lifecycle
Scheduler creates task_instance
Assigned to user
App shows: “Next Task”
User completes via:
voice → /voice/complete
or photo → /tasks/:id/evidence
System validates:
timing
penalty level
verification requirement
Marks complete OR requests proof
B. Trust update
On completion:
write trust_events
update user_stats_daily
recalc EWMA trust score
adjust trust_tier + penalty_level
C. Anti-cheat trigger
If signals hit threshold:
set penalty_level = warning/penalty
modify future tasks:
require photo
disable batching
D. Reward engine
On each clean completion:
increment counters
check thresholds
create reward_earnings (pending)
auto-approve if rules allow
4) 2–4 WEEK MVP BUILD PLAN
Week 1
Auth + org + users
Task creation + assignment
Basic UI (Next Task screen)
Manual completion
Week 2
Voice → text integration
Sequential task flow
Basic trust score (no EWMA yet)
Manager dashboard (simple)
Week 3
Photo upload + storage
Penalty levels (warning/penalty)
Reward counters (simple streaks)
Week 4
Full trust formula + EWMA
Anti-cheat signals
Rewards (break + meal)
Notifications (due/overdue)
Ship here.
5) PRICING — REFINED (WHAT WILL ACTUALLY SELL)
Positioning
Price it as: “Reduce labor waste + enforce standards”
Not “checklists” or “training.”
Recommended structure
Starter — $99/location/month
up to 10 employees
core task system
voice completion
basic trust score
limited analytics
👉 goal: easy entry
Pro — $249/location/month (MOST IMPORTANT)
up to 30–40 employees
SOP generation
anti-cheat system
photo verification
rewards system
full analytics
notifications + escalation
👉 this is your main revenue plan
Multi-location — $599–$1,499/month
unlimited employees per location tier
centralized SOPs
cross-location analytics
advanced permissions
priority support
👉 target franchises
Usage pricing (AI)
Keep it simple:
include 500–1,000 transcription minutes
overage: ~$0.01–$0.02/min
Do NOT overcomplicate early.
Add-ons (later)
AI photo validation → +$29–$99/month
advanced analytics → +$49/month
integrations (POS/HR) → enterprise
6) PRICING STRATEGY (IMPORTANT)
Anchor value
If you save:
1 bad shift/week
or 1 hour/employee/week
You’re easily worth: 👉 $200–$500/month/location
Sales angle
Don’t say: “Task management app”
Say: “We make sure your staff actually does what they’re supposed to do.”

[Once connected:]
Hi, my name is Daniel. I know this might seem random, but I came across your shop a couple of weeks ago when I had to help my girlfriend fix her brakes.
Basically, I’ll put it straightforward: you lost me as a customer for around $500.
Would you like to hear the reason why?
[If they say yes:]
So I found another auto shop with a website that had basic price ranges for common repairs. Their range for the brake work was lower than the two quotes I had already gotten over the phone, so I called them just to confirm it applied to her car.
Honestly, having that information on the website made it feel easier to trust them in that moment. I did not have to call another shop completely blind and start the whole conversation over again.
And now that I’ve been looking at auto shops more, I’ve noticed a lot of them do not have websites at all. Usually, when I’m looking into any business, the first place I go is their website.
When I could not find one for you guys, it just threw me off a little and made me choose the other place instead.
So the reason I’m calling is that I build websites for local businesses as a side job.
Before you think I’m just calling to sell you something, I usually work with bars, restaurants, and places like that. I’ve never actually designed one for an auto shop before.
But after dealing with my girlfriend’s situation, it made me realize something.
For a restaurant, a website has to bring in a lot of extra customers before it really pays for itself. But for an auto shop, it could take just one customer.
If one person finds your shop, trusts the website enough to call you, and brings in one decent repair job, that could cover the entire cost of the website. Every customer after that is basically extra money the website brought in for you.
And I charge extremely reasonable prices compared to regular web-design companies because I freelance independently. I’m not splitting the price between a salesperson, project manager, designer, and a bunch of other people.
Before you say no, I already made a website concept for your shop.
It’s 100% free to look at. It’s basically just the design and layout I came up with based on your business.
It costs you nothing to check it out. I can send you the link just so you can see it.
If you like it and think it could help your shop, we can talk about moving forward from there. If not, no problem at all — I just wanted to show you what I had in mind.
Even if it’s not something you want to move forward with, I’d still genuinely appreciate your opinion on the design.
I don’t take feedback personally. If there’s something about it that you don’t like, or something that would make it more useful for an auto shop, that feedback could help me make a better version for a future client.

    fontSize: "13px",
    padding: "12px",
    outline: "none",
    boxSizing: "border-box",
  },
  tagRow: {
    display: "flex",
    flexWrap: "wrap",
    gap: "8px",
    marginTop: "4px",
  },
  tag: (active) => ({
    padding: "6px 14px",
    borderRadius: "999px",
    fontSize: "12px",
    cursor: "pointer",
    border: `1px solid ${active ? ACCENT : BORDER}`,
    background: active ? `${ACCENT}15` : "transparent",
    color: active ? ACCENT : MUTED,
    transition: "all 0.15s",
    letterSpacing: "0.05em",
  }),
  btn: (disabled) => ({
    width: "100%",
    padding: "16px",
    background: disabled ? "#1a1a2e" : ACCENT,
    color: disabled ? MUTED : "#000",
    border: "none",
    borderRadius: "10px",
    fontSize: "13px",
    fontWeight: "700",
    letterSpacing: "0.15em",
    textTransform: "uppercase",
    cursor: disabled ? "not-allowed" : "pointer",
    fontFamily: "'DM Mono', monospace",
    transition: "all 0.2s",
    marginTop: "8px",
  }),
  progress: {
    background: BG,
    border: `1px solid ${BORDER}`,
    borderRadius: "8px",
    padding: "20px 24px",
    marginBottom: "20px",
  },
  progressBar: (pct) => ({
    height: "3px",
    background: `linear-gradient(90deg, ${ACCENT} ${pct}%, ${BORDER} ${pct}%)`,
    borderRadius: "2px",
    marginTop: "10px",
    transition: "background 0.3s",
  }),
  clipCard: (i) => ({
    background: SURFACE,
    border: `1px solid ${BORDER}`,
    borderRadius: "10px",
    padding: "18px 20px",
    marginBottom: "12px",
    display: "flex",
    gap: "16px",
    alignItems: "flex-start",
    animation: `fadeUp 0.3s ease both`,
    animationDelay: `${i * 60}ms`,
  }),
  timestamp: {
    background: `${ACCENT}18`,
    border: `1px solid ${ACCENT}40`,
    borderRadius: "6px",
    padding: "4px 10px",
    fontSize: "12px",
    color: ACCENT,
    whiteSpace: "nowrap",
    fontWeight: "600",
    letterSpacing: "0.05em",
  },
  clipTitle: {
    fontSize: "14px",
    fontWeight: "600",
    fontFamily: "'Space Grotesk', sans-serif",
    marginBottom: "4px",
    color: TEXT,
  },
  clipDesc: {
    fontSize: "12px",
    color: MUTED,
    lineHeight: "1.5",
  },
  scoreBadge: (score) => ({
    marginLeft: "auto",
    fontSize: "11px",
    color: score >= 8 ? ACCENT : score >= 6 ? "#fbbf24" : MUTED,
    border: `1px solid ${score >= 8 ? ACCENT + "40" : score >= 6 ? "#fbbf2440" : BORDER}`,
    borderRadius: "4px",
    padding: "2px 8px",
    whiteSpace: "nowrap",
  }),
  dropzone: (dragging) => ({
    border: `2px dashed ${dragging ? ACCENT : BORDER}`,
    borderRadius: "10px",
    padding: "32px",
    textAlign: "center",
    cursor: "pointer",
    background: dragging ? `${ACCENT}08` : "transparent",
    transition: "all 0.2s",
    color: MUTED,
    fontSize: "13px",
  }),
  tabRow: {
    display: "flex",
    gap: "0",
    marginBottom: "16px",
    borderRadius: "8px",
    overflow: "hidden",
    border: `1px solid ${BORDER}`,
    width: "fit-content",
  },
  tab: (active) => ({
    padding: "8px 20px",
    fontSize: "12px",
    letterSpacing: "0.1em",
    cursor: "pointer",
    background: active ? ACCENT : "transparent",
    color: active ? "#000" : MUTED,
    border: "none",
    fontFamily: "'DM Mono', monospace",
    fontWeight: active ? "700" : "400",
    transition: "all 0.15s",
    textTransform: "uppercase",
  }),
};

const DEFAULT_SIGNALS = [
  "Loud reaction / screaming",
  "Funny moment",
  "Dramatic tension",
  "Unexpected twist",
  "Hype / peak energy",
  "Emotional moment",
];

const PRESET_SIGNALS = {
  Gaming: ["Kill streak / big play", "Rage moment", "Clutch win", "Funny fail"],
  Sports: ["Big hit / goal", "Crowd eruption", "Controversial call", "Comeback moment"],
  Talk: ["Shocking statement", "Heated debate", "Burst of laughter", "Vulnerable moment"],
};

function formatTime(sec) {
  const m = Math.floor(sec / 60).toString().padStart(2, "0");
  const s = (sec % 60).toString().padStart(2, "0");
  return `${m}:${s}`;
}

export default function HighlightDetector() {
  const [tab, setTab] = useState("upload");
  const [videoUrl, setVideoUrl] = useState("");
  const [videoFile, setVideoFile] = useState(null);
  const [signals, setSignals] = useState(["Loud reaction / screaming", "Funny moment", "Hype / peak energy"]);
  const [customSignal, setCustomSignal] = useState("");
  const [customInstructions, setCustomInstructions] = useState("");
  const [dragging, setDragging] = useState(false);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);
  const [progress, setProgress] = useState(0);
  const [progressMsg, setProgressMsg] = useState("");
  const [clips, setClips] = useState(null);
  const [error, setError] = useState("");
  const [videoDuration, setVideoDuration] = useState(null);
  const fileRef = useRef();
  const videoRef = useRef();

  const toggleSignal = (s) => {
    setSignals((prev) =>
      prev.includes(s) ? prev.filter((x) => x !== s) : [...prev, s]
    );
  };

  const addCustom = () => {
    const trimmed = customSignal.trim();
    if (trimmed && !signals.includes(trimmed)) {
      setSignals((prev) => [...prev, trimmed]);
      setCustomSignal("");
    }
  };

  const handleDrop = useCallback((e) => {
    e.preventDefault();
    setDragging(false);
    const file = e.dataTransfer.files[0];
    if (file && file.type.startsWith("video/")) setVideoFile(file);
  }, []);

  const handleFileChange = (e) => {
    const file = e.target.files[0];
    if (file) setVideoFile(file);
  };

  const onVideoLoad = (e) => {
    setVideoDuration(Math.floor(e.target.duration));
  };

  const simulate = async () => {
    setLoading(true);
    setClips(null);
    setError("");
    setProgress(0);

    const steps = [
      [10, "Ingesting video..."],
      [30, "Analyzing audio peaks..."],
      [55, "Scanning for visual cues..."],
      [75, "Running AI highlight detection..."],
      [90, "Scoring moments..."],
      [100, "Finalizing clips..."],
    ];

    for (const [pct, msg] of steps) {
      setProgress(pct);
      setProgressMsg(msg);
      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 600 + Math.random() * 400));
    }

    const duration = videoDuration || 900;
    const signalList = signals.join(", ");
    const videoLabel = videoFile ? videoFile.name : videoUrl || "the provided video";
    const extra = customInstructions ? `\nExtra context: ${customInstructions}` : "";

    const prompt = `You are a professional video clip analyst. Analyze "${videoLabel}" (duration: ~${Math.floor(duration / 60)} minutes) and identify the TOP 6-10 highlight moments.

The user wants clips triggered by these signals: ${signalList}.${extra}

For each highlight, provide:
- A realistic timestamp (spread across the video duration of ${duration} seconds)
- A short punchy title (5 words max)
- A 1-sentence description of what happens
- A viral score from 1-10

Return ONLY valid JSON array. No markdown. No explanation. Example:
[{"start":45,"end":75,"title":"Insane clutch moment","description":"Player pulls off a 1v5 with seconds left on the clock.","score":9},...]

Generate 7 clips spread realistically across the video. Make them feel real and specific.`;

    try {
      const resp = await fetch("https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages", {
        method: "POST",
        headers: {
          "Content-Type": "application/json",
          "anthropic-version": "2023-06-01",
          "anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access": "true",
        },
        body: JSON.stringify({
          model: "claude-sonnet-4-20250514",
          max_tokens: 1000,
          messages: [{ role: "user", content: prompt }],
        }),
      });

      if (!resp.ok) {
        const errText = await resp.text();
        setError(`API error ${resp.status}: ${errText.slice(0, 200)}`);
        setLoading(false);
        return;
      }

      const data = await resp.json();
      const raw = data.content?.map((b) => b.text || "").join("") || "";
      const clean = raw.replace(/```json|```/g, "").trim();

      // Find JSON array in response even if there's surrounding text
      const match = clean.match(/\[[\s\S]*\]/);
      if (!match) {
        setError(`No clip data found in response. Raw: ${raw.slice(0, 300)}`);
        setLoading(false);
        return;
      }

      const parsed = JSON.parse(match[0]);
      setClips(parsed);
    } catch (err) {
      setError(`Error: ${err.message}`);
    }

    setLoading(false);
  };

  const canRun = signals.length > 0 && (videoFile || videoUrl.trim().length > 5);

  return (
    <div style={styles.app}>
      <style>{`
        @import url('https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=DM+Mono:wght@400;500&family=Space+Grotesk:wght@400;600;700&display=swap');
        * { box-sizing: border-box; }
        @keyframes pulse { 0%,100%{opacity:1} 50%{opacity:0.3} }
        @keyframes fadeUp { from{opacity:0;transform:translateY(10px)} to{opacity:1;transform:translateY(0)} }
        textarea:focus, input:focus { border-color: #00ff8760 !important; }
        ::-webkit-scrollbar { width: 4px; } ::-webkit-scrollbar-track { background: #0a0a0f; } ::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb { background: #1e1e2e; border-radius: 2px; }
      `}</style>

      <div style={styles.header}>
        <div style={styles.dot} />
        <span style={styles.logo}>ClipAI — Highlight Detector</span>
      </div>

      <div style={styles.main}>
        <h1 style={styles.h1}>Find every<br />viral moment.</h1>
        <p style={styles.sub}>// AI-powered highlight detection · drop a video · get your clips</p>

        {/* VIDEO INPUT */}
        <div style={styles.card}>
          <span style={styles.label}>01 — Video Source</span>
          <div style={styles.tabRow}>
            <button style={styles.tab(tab === "upload")} onClick={() => setTab("upload")}>Upload</button>
            <button style={styles.tab(tab === "url")} onClick={() => setTab("url")}>URL</button>
          </div>

          {tab === "upload" ? (
            <div
              style={styles.dropzone(dragging)}
              onClick={() => fileRef.current.click()}
              onDragOver={(e) => { e.preventDefault(); setDragging(true); }}
              onDragLeave={() => setDragging(false)}
              onDrop={handleDrop}
            >
              <input ref={fileRef} type="file" accept="video/*" style={{ display: "none" }} onChange={handleFileChange} />
              {videoFile ? (
                <span style={{ color: ACCENT }}>✓ {videoFile.name}</span>
              ) : (
                <>Drop video file here or click to browse<br /><span style={{ fontSize: "11px", opacity: 0.5, marginTop: "4px", display: "block" }}>MP4, MOV, MKV, WebM supported</span></>
              )}
              {videoFile && (
                <video ref={videoRef} src={URL.createObjectURL(videoFile)} onLoadedMetadata={onVideoLoad} style={{ display: "none" }} />
              )}
            </div>
          ) : (
            <input
              style={styles.input}
              placeholder="https://youtube.com/watch?v=... or any video URL"
              value={videoUrl}
              onChange={(e) => setVideoUrl(e.target.value)}
            />
          )}
        </div>

        {/* SIGNALS */}
        <div style={styles.card}>
          <span style={styles.label}>02 — What to look for</span>
          <div style={styles.tagRow}>
            {[...DEFAULT_SIGNALS, ...Object.values(PRESET_SIGNALS).flat()].filter((v, i, a) => a.indexOf(v) === i).map((s) => (
              <button key={s} style={styles.tag(signals.includes(s))} onClick={() => toggleSignal(s)}>{s}</button>
            ))}
          </div>
          <div style={{ display: "flex", gap: "8px", marginTop: "14px" }}>
            <input
              style={{ ...styles.input, flex: 1 }}
              placeholder="Add custom signal..."
              value={customSignal}
              onChange={(e) => setCustomSignal(e.target.value)}
              onKeyDown={(e) => e.key === "Enter" && addCustom()}
            />
            <button
              onClick={addCustom}
              style={{ padding: "10px 16px", background: `${ACCENT}20`, border: `1px solid ${ACCENT}40`, color: ACCENT, borderRadius: "8px", cursor: "pointer", fontSize: "12px", fontFamily: "'DM Mono', monospace" }}
            >+ Add</button>
          </div>
          {signals.length > 0 && (
            <div style={{ marginTop: "12px", fontSize: "11px", color: MUTED }}>
              Active: {signals.map(s => (
                <span key={s} style={{ marginRight: "8px", color: ACCENT }}>{s} <span style={{ cursor: "pointer", opacity: 0.5 }} onClick={() => toggleSignal(s)}>×</span></span>
              ))}
            </div>
          )}
        </div>

        {/* EXTRA CONTEXT */}
        <div style={styles.card}>
          <span style={styles.label}>03 — Extra Context (optional)</span>
          <textarea
            style={styles.textarea}
            placeholder="e.g. 'This is a Valorant stream, the streamer is known for rage moments and funny commentary. Focus on moments that would make good Shorts.'"
            value={customInstructions}
            onChange={(e) => setCustomInstructions(e.target.value)}
          />
        </div>

        {/* RUN */}
        <button style={styles.btn(!canRun)} disabled={!canRun} onClick={simulate}>
          {loading ? "Analyzing..." : "▶ Detect Highlights"}
        </button>

        {/* PROGRESS */}
        {loading && (
          <div style={styles.progress}>
            <div style={{ display: "flex", justifyContent: "space-between", fontSize: "12px" }}>
              <span style={{ color: ACCENT }}>{progressMsg}</span>
              <span style={{ color: MUTED }}>{progress}%</span>
            </div>
            <div style={styles.progressBar(progress)} />
          </div>
        )}

        {error && (
          <div style={{ ...styles.card, borderColor: "#ef444440", color: "#ef4444", fontSize: "13px" }}>{error}</div>
        )}

        {/* RESULTS */}
        {clips && clips.length > 0 && (
          <div style={{ marginTop: "32px" }}>
            <div style={{ display: "flex", justifyContent: "space-between", alignItems: "center", marginBottom: "16px" }}>
              <span style={{ ...styles.label, margin: 0 }}>// {clips.length} highlights detected</span>
              <span style={{ fontSize: "11px", color: MUTED }}>sorted by timestamp</span>
            </div>
            {clips.sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start).map((clip, i) => (
              <div key={i} style={styles.clipCard(i)}>
                <div>
                  <span style={styles.timestamp}>{formatTime(clip.start)} → {formatTime(clip.end)}</span>
                </div>
                <div style={{ flex: 1 }}>
                  <div style={styles.clipTitle}>{clip.title}</div>
                  <div style={styles.clipDesc}>{clip.description}</div>
                  <div style={{ marginTop: "6px", fontSize: "11px", color: MUTED }}>
                    Duration: {clip.end - clip.start}s
                  </div>
                </div>
                <div style={styles.scoreBadge(clip.score)}>
                  {clip.score}/10
                </div>
              </div>
            ))}
            <div style={{ marginTop: "20px", padding: "16px", background: `${ACCENT}08`, border: `1px solid ${ACCENT}20`, borderRadius: "10px", fontSize: "12px", color: MUTED, lineHeight: "1.6" }}>
              <span style={{ color: ACCENT }}>// Next steps:</span> Scrub to each timestamp in your editor · Keep clips scored 7+ · Trim to 30–60 seconds · Add captions before posting
            </div>
          </div>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );

You are a social media strategist specializing in rapid Instagram growth for local nightlife and entertainment venues.
Your goal is to generate high-performing faceless Instagram content ideas for a karaoke bar. The audience is people looking for fun nightlife experiences, birthdays, group outings, and casual nights out with friends.
All content must be faceless. Use visuals such as:
• neon lights
• karaoke rooms
• microphones
• drinks being poured
• song screens
• people entering rooms (no faces shown)
• close-ups of hands holding microphones
• crowd silhouettes
• drinks clinking
• room lighting and atmosphere
Do not rely on talking heads.

Google needs a list of your existing customers first, then it can build a similar audience from them.
This is called Customer Match.
Below is the step-by-step process.
1. Build a customer email list
Google needs a list of past customers.
Create a spreadsheet with:
Email
Phone (optional but helpful)
First Name (optional)
Last Name (optional)
Example:
Email
First Name
Last Name
john@email.com
John
Perez
maria@email.com
Maria
Lopez
You want at least 100–300 contacts for good results.
Save the file as CSV.
2. Upload the list to Google Ads
Go to:
https://ads.google.com⁠�
Steps:
Click Tools & Settings
Click Shared Library
Click Audience Manager
Click Segments
Click +
Select Customer List
Upload your CSV file.
Google will match those emails with Google users.
3. Google builds similar audiences
Once your list is uploaded, Google analyzes:
• search behavior
• locations
• interests
• nightlife activity
• group events
It then creates people who behave like your customers.
For example, Google may find people who:
• search “birthday party near me”
• search “karaoke queens”
• visit nightlife websites
• attend group events
4. Use that audience in ads
When running ads you can choose:
Audience targeting
Example audiences Google might build:
• Similar to your past customers
• People searching karaoke
• Birthday planners
• Local nightlife groups
This dramatically improves ad quality.
5. Add location targeting
For a karaoke venue this is critical.
Set ads to:
Queens
Flushing
Bayside
Nassau County
NYC
Distance radius: 10–20 miles
This prevents wasted ad spend.
6. Use conversion signals
Google gets smarter when it sees bookings.
Install a conversion tag on your website.
When someone books:
Google learns what type of person converts.
Over time it finds more people like them.
Example result
Instead of ads going to random people, Google might target:
• people planning birthdays
• groups searching nightlife
• people who visited karaoke websites
• people similar to your previous customers
This dramatically increases booking chances.
What size list works best
Minimum: about 100 emails
Good: 500+ emails
Great: 1000+ emails
Even smaller lists can still help.
If you want, I can also show you:
• a powerful Google Ads strategy specifically for karaoke venues
• how to find people planning birthdays (your most profitable customers)
• how to recover customers who visited your site but didn’t book.If you want Google to find similar customers, you’re really talking about using Google Ads audience targeting. Google needs a list of your existing customers first, then it can build a similar audience from them.

Save-Optimized Carousel Generator
PROMPT (Use in ChatGPT or Claude):
Generate 12 Instagram carousel concepts specifically designed to maximize saves and shares. Each concept must have high education density and clear practical value. Include structured formats such as step-by-step blueprints, framework breakdowns, checklists, common mistakes, myths vs truth comparisons, before-and-after transformations, case studies, and swipe-to-learn series. Each idea should be designed to increase reading time and screenshot intent while positioning me as an authority in my niche.
Saves signal value.
Shares expand reach.

Caption Engine
PROMPT (Use in ChatGPT or Claude):
Rewrite this caption using emotional storytelling and simple, conversational language. Keep readability at grade 6–7 level. Add subtle authority positioning without sounding salesy. Include a save trigger, a comment trigger, and a clear but natural call to action. Integrate friendly keywords relevant to my niche. The caption should feel human, reflective, and psychologically engaging, not robotic or overly structured.
Captions reinforce emotion.
Emotion increases action.

Hook Generator (Who / What / How Framework)
PROMPT (Use in ChatGPT or Claude):
Generate 20 high-retention Instagram hooks for my niche. Every hook must clearly answer three questions within the first line: Who is this for? What is this about? How does this help them? The hook must instantly communicate clarity and value. Use a mix of pattern interrupts, authority positioning, contrarian angles, curiosity gaps, and outcome-driven framing. Avoid vague or clever phrasing. The goal is immediate relevance and psychological pull, not creativity for its own sake.
Who creates clarity.
What builds context.
How delivers value. Miss one and retention drops.

Content Strategy Architect
PROMPT (Use in ChatGPT or Claude):
Create a 30-day Instagram content strategy for my niche. Define 4 to 5 core content pillars aligned with audience pain points and desired outcomes. Map the audience journey from awareness to trust, authority, and conversion. Recommend how to sequence topics strategically so each post builds on the previous one. Specify when to educate, when to share stories, when to build authority, and when to introduce offers. Allocate formats intentionally between reels, carousels, and stories. This must feel like a structured growth blueprint, not a random posting calendar.
Consistency alone doesn’t scale.
Structured sequencing does.

Research Engine (Content Intelligence Layer)
PROMPT (Use in ChatGPT or Claude):
Act as a senior Instagram content strategist and behavioral analyst. Study the top-performing posts in my niche and reverse engineer why they worked. Analyze at least 10 viral posts and identify recurring hook structures, emotional triggers, positioning angles, psychological drivers behind saves and shares, call-to-action timing, and topic repetition patterns. Explain how attention is captured, how retention is maintained, and what creates conversion. Identify content gaps competitors are missing and suggest strategic opportunities I can leverage to stand out. Present this as applied insights I can directly implement, not surface observations.
This turns AI into a content intelligence system.
You stop copying trends and start decoding patterns.

High-Retention Script Builder
PROMPT (Use in ChatGPT or Claude):
Write a high-retention Instagram script on this topic. Before writing, choose the most psychologically effective persuasion framework for maximum retention. You may use AIDA, PAS, Value-Delayed Framework, Open Loop Technique, Story-Bridge-Offer, Before-After-Bridge, or the 4U Framework. Select the framework intentionally based on the topic and explain the structural flow internally before drafting. The script must increase completion rate, create tension before delivering value, and build curiosity that sustains attention until the final line.
Structure creates completion.
Completion drives distribution.
If you want, I can also convert these into one master prompt that automatically generates all of this for your karaoke bar content (hooks, reels, captions, and a 30-day strategy). That would let you create weeks of content in one command.

Viral Content Generation Prompt (Instagram / Reels / Short-form Video)
Use this when generating daily social media content.
Prompt:
You are a nightlife content strategist specializing in viral social media growth for bars and entertainment venues.
Your job is to create high-engagement Instagram and short-form video content ideas for a karaoke bar called Christmas Karaoke located in Queens, New York.
Business context:
Christmas Karaoke is a large private-room karaoke venue with 15 rooms, a full bar, and a huge song library. The venue is cozy and casual but large enough for big parties. Customers mainly come for birthdays, friend groups, and celebrations.
Rooms typically cost about $10 per person per hour with packages from $21–$45 per person, saving customers roughly $15+ per person. Birthday groups booking packages receive a free bottle of champagne.
The goal of the content is to:
• increase brand awareness
• attract birthday parties and friend groups
• increase weekday bookings
• make viewers want to visit immediately
Content should prioritize high retention and shareability.
For every idea include:
Hook (first 2 seconds of the video)
Video concept
Suggested on-screen text
Caption optimized for SEO
Call-to-action
Why this video will perform well
Focus on formats such as:
• surprising karaoke moments
• funny karaoke situations
• POV nightlife experiences
• relatable karaoke humor
• group celebration energy
• birthday party content
• “things you didn't know about karaoke bars”
• nightlife hacks
Videos should be designed to stop scrolling immediately.
Avoid generic nightlife content. Make the videos feel authentic to a Queens karaoke bar environment.

Prompt:
You are a local SEO strategist specializing in Google Maps ranking for nightlife venues and bars in New York City.
Your goal is to help a karaoke venue called Christmas Karaoke in Queens, New York rank higher in Google Maps searches for terms such as:
• karaoke near me
• karaoke Queens

Christmas Karaoke is a large karaoke venue with 15 private rooms, a full bar, and one of the largest song libraries in the area. Rooms average around $10 per person per hour and packages range $21–$45 per person.
Birthday groups booking packages receive a free bottle of champagne.
Marketing channels currently driving traffic:
• Google Maps • Instagram
The business goal is to increase organic Google discovery traffic.
When providing advice, focus on:
• Google Business Profile optimization • review strategies • keyword optimization • Google post strategies • photo strategies • local backlinks • ranking signals specific to NYC nightlife businesses
Provide:
• step-by-step actions • example Google posts • review request scripts • SEO optimized descriptions • keyword strategies
Prioritize strategies that increase visibility for karaoke searches in Queens and nearby neighborhoods.
Avoid generic SEO advice. Focus specifically on Google Maps ranking factors.

The pattern I noticed is that whenever you blame me for a problem, I look into the numbers to see what I would need to fix. But when I check the actual cause, the problem usually comes from decisions being made without doing the math first.
For example, when you said expenses were high because I was giving service, I calculated what I would actually have to give away for that to make a serious difference. The amount would be unrealistic: dozens of beers, many bottles of soju, or a huge number of shots or mixed drinks. That means the expense problem is not being explained correctly.
The same thing happened when you said BYOB was more profitable than packages. I believed there was a chance you were right because you have been in this business for many years. But when I looked at it, the numbers did not support that.
Food is another example. When you said we should stop making food because it was costing money, that was also said without a full calculation of cost, sales, customer value, and whether food helps customers stay longer or spend more.
The same problem happens with advertising. Google Ads should be bringing in new customers. Instagram and package ads should be tested based on actual sales, reservations, and customer response. One Instagram post without ads already brought in package sales. Instead of testing it properly, the idea was dismissed too quickly because expenses were high. But when sales are low, that is exactly when marketing needs to be used correctly.
I also do not think it is fair to say customers do not come when I work. Last week, my sales were higher than Sula’s sales. When I averaged the weekday numbers, my average was higher. So the “bad luck” explanation does not match the sales data.
The bigger problem is that decisions are being made based on assumptions instead of numbers. If we want the store to improve, we need to stop guessing and start tracking sales, expenses, ads, packages, BYOB, food, and staff performance properly.
I am not saying this to argue. I am saying this because the store keeps losing opportunities when decisions are made emotionally or based on old habits instead of actual numbers.[  ] Karaoke app with lic

This Confidentiality, Non-Disclosure, Non-Use, Non-Circumvention, and Intellectual Property Assignment Agreement “Agreement” is entered into as of ________, 20 “Effective Date”, by and between:
Owner / Disclosing Party:
Name: __________________________________________
Address: ________________________________________
Email: __________________________________________
and
Receiving Party:
Name: __________________________________________
Address: ________________________________________
Email: __________________________________________
The Owner and the Receiving Party may each be referred to as a “Party” and collectively as the “Parties.”
1. Purpose
The Owner may disclose, has disclosed, or may provide access to confidential, proprietary, technical, creative, business, operational, financial, software, product, and intellectual property information in connection with evaluating, discussing, testing, developing, improving, advising on, investing in, partnering on, or otherwise working with the Owner’s apps, websites, software, business systems, digital products, service-business tools, hospitality systems, AI workflows, automation systems, or related business opportunities.
The purpose of this Agreement is to protect the Owner’s Confidential Information, trade secrets, Work Product, business opportunities, and intellectual property from unauthorized disclosure, use, copying, development, reverse engineering, circumvention, misappropriation, or exploitation.
2. Consideration
The Receiving Party acknowledges that good and valuable consideration supports this Agreement, including one or more of the following:
a. access to Confidential Information;
b. continued access to Confidential Information previously disclosed;
c. access to business opportunities, product discussions, demonstrations, prototypes, code, accounts, designs, workflows, or technical systems;
d. the opportunity to evaluate a potential business, employment, contractor, investment, advisory, cofounder, partnership, or development relationship with the Owner;
e. the Owner’s willingness to continue discussions or provide further information after the Effective Date.
The Receiving Party acknowledges that this consideration is sufficient and that the Owner is relying on this Agreement before making further disclosures or continuing access.
3. Prior Disclosures
This Agreement applies to Confidential Information disclosed before, on, or after the Effective Date.
The Receiving Party acknowledges that any Confidential Information previously received from the Owner remains confidential and is covered by this Agreement in exchange for the consideration described above, including continued access, continued discussions, and the opportunity to evaluate or continue a business relationship with the Owner.
No prior disclosure shall be deemed public, unrestricted, waived, or unprotected merely because it occurred before this Agreement was signed.
4. Definition of Confidential Information
“Confidential Information” means all non-public information, materials, systems, concepts, business assets, technical assets, creative assets, trade secrets, and intellectual property disclosed by, owned by, controlled by, developed by, planned by, or created for the Owner.
Confidential Information includes information disclosed verbally, visually, electronically, physically, through demonstrations, screenshots, prototypes, messages, meetings, calls, source-code access, admin access, database access, AI-tool access, design-file access, documents, notes, recordings, credentials, or any other method.
Confidential Information includes, without limitation:
a. apps, websites, SaaS products, software platforms, dashboards, internal tools, admin panels, customer portals, ordering systems, hospitality systems, restaurant systems, bar systems, karaoke systems, venue-management systems, service-business systems, employee systems, manager systems, customer systems, reservation systems, menu systems, voice systems, SOP systems, marketing systems, automation systems, and AI systems;
b. source code, object code, scripts, repositories, databases, schemas, APIs, API keys, prompts, system prompts, AI-agent instructions, automation flows, backend logic, frontend logic, authentication flows, hosting details, server details, cloud configurations, data structures, algorithms, technical architecture, bug reports, deployment notes, implementation methods, and testing notes;
c. user interfaces, wireframes, prototypes, Figma files, Canva files, screenshots, layouts, page structures, design systems, logos, brand assets, copywriting, graphics, animations, videos, ads, landing pages, user flows, product flows, menu structures, feature logic, button labels, and customer-facing content;
d. business ideas, startup ideas, monetization plans, subscription models, pricing strategies, sales strategies, SEO strategies, ad strategies, customer-acquisition plans, investor materials, financial projections, vendor lists, customer lists, prospect lists, employee information, contractor information, operating procedures, analytics, sales data, market-positioning strategies, pitch materials, and business records;
e. business names, app names, product names, domain names, brand names, slogans, trademarks, social media handles, product categories, product positioning, roadmaps, planned features, planned improvements, and concrete future development plans;
f. Confidential Information relating to products, apps, software, systems, workflows, businesses, features, or projects that the Owner has created, is creating, is actively evaluating, has documented, has demonstrated, has discussed with the Receiving Party, or later develops based on the same confidential subject matter disclosed to the Receiving Party;
g. all notes, copies, summaries, screenshots, photos, recordings, exports, downloads, backups, files, sketches, diagrams, reproductions, modifications, improvements, derivative works, or materials created by the Receiving Party that contain, reflect, summarize, are based on, are influenced by, or are derived from Confidential Information.
Confidential Information does not need to be marked “confidential” to be protected.
5. Trade Secrets
Some Confidential Information may also constitute trade secrets under applicable law.
Trade secrets include information that derives actual or potential independent economic value from not being generally known and that is subject to reasonable efforts to maintain secrecy.
Trade secrets may include source code, databases, business workflows, customer information, product strategy, pricing strategy, AI workflows, internal operating procedures, technical architecture, unreleased products, unreleased features, prompt systems, software logic, and non-public business methods.
Trade secrets shall remain protected for as long as they remain trade secrets under applicable law.
6. Confidentiality Obligations
The Receiving Party shall hold all Confidential Information in strict confidence and shall use at least the same degree of care used to protect the Receiving Party’s own confidential information, but in no event less than reasonable care.
The Receiving Party shall not directly or indirectly disclose, reveal, publish, post, describe, summarize, demonstrate, show, transfer, transmit, distribute, sell, license, leak, upload, expose, or communicate Confidential Information to any person or entity without the Owner’s prior written consent.
This prohibition applies to disclosure in whole or in part, including disclosure in modified, anonymized, renamed, rebranded, summarized, generalized, or partial form if the disclosure could reasonably allow another person to identify, reconstruct, use, or benefit from the Confidential Information.
7. Non-Use
The Receiving Party shall not use Confidential Information for any purpose except the specific authorized purpose stated in Exhibit C or otherwise approved in a signed writing by the Owner.
Without limiting the foregoing, the Receiving Party shall not use Confidential Information to:
a. build, improve, copy, modify, test, launch, market, sell, license, or commercialize any app, website, software, SaaS product, platform, automation, AI system, workflow, database, dashboard, service, or business;
b. assist any third party in building, improving, copying, modifying, testing, launching, marketing, selling, licensing, or commercializing any app, website, software, SaaS product, platform, automation, AI system, workflow, database, dashboard, service, or business;
c. compete with the Owner through use of Confidential Information;
d. create a product, feature, workflow, design, technical system, or business method based on, derived from, or enabled by Confidential Information;
e. file or assist with any intellectual property application based on Confidential Information;
f. register or purchase any domain name, business name, trademark, social media handle, app name, product name, or brand asset based on Confidential Information;
g. obtain employment, contracts, customers, investors, partners, vendors, developers, designers, business opportunities, or financial benefit through use of Confidential Information;
h. train, fine-tune, prompt, instruct, develop, or improve any AI model, AI agent, automation, software tool, coding tool, or design tool.
This section restricts misuse of Confidential Information. It does not prohibit the Receiving Party from using general skills, knowledge, and experience lawfully acquired without use of, reliance on, or reference to the Owner’s Confidential Information.
8. No Copying, Recording, Downloading, or Screenshotting
Unless expressly authorized in writing by the Owner, the Receiving Party shall not copy, photograph, screenshot, screen record, audio record, video record, download, export, print, save, duplicate, back up, scrape, clone, mirror, archive, reconstruct, preserve, store, or transfer any Confidential Information.
Any unauthorized copy, screenshot, note, file, export, recording, summary, or backup is deemed Confidential Information and property of the Owner.
9. No Reverse Engineering or Reconstruction
The Receiving Party shall not directly or indirectly reverse engineer, decompile, disassemble, inspect, model, reconstruct, rebuild, clone, imitate, adapt, modify, improve upon, create derivative works from, create functionally equivalent versions of, or create substantially similar versions of any Confidential Information, app, website, software, workflow, system, code, database, design, product concept, business process, or technical process belonging to the Owner.
If the Receiving Party had access to Confidential Information and later creates, contributes to, assists with, or benefits from a product, system, workflow, feature, design, codebase, or business method that is substantially similar to the Owner’s Confidential Information, that similarity may be considered evidence that the later work was based on or derived from the Owner’s Confidential Information.
This section does not prevent a court or arbitrator from evaluating all facts and evidence, but the Receiving Party agrees that memory-based reconstruction, note-based reconstruction, screenshot-based reconstruction, or demonstration-based reconstruction is prohibited when based on or derived from Confidential Information.
10. No AI Tool or Third-Party Platform Exposure
The Receiving Party shall not enter, upload, paste, transmit, submit, sync, connect, process, store, or expose Confidential Information into any AI tool, coding tool, design tool, cloud tool, automation tool, file-sharing tool, repository, project-management tool, or third-party platform without the Owner’s prior written consent.
This includes, without limitation, ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Grok, Perplexity, Codex, Cursor, Replit, Lovable, Bolt, V0, GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket, Vercel, Netlify, Supabase, Firebase, Figma, Canva, Wix, Google Drive, Dropbox, Notion, Slack, Discord, Trello, Asana, Jira, or any similar platform.
If the Owner authorizes use of any third-party platform, the Receiving Party shall use only accounts, workspaces, repositories, permissions, and access methods approved by the Owner.
11. No Unauthorized Contractors, Assistants, or Delegation
The Receiving Party shall not involve, hire, consult, instruct, outsource to, or delegate work to any employee, contractor, developer, designer, consultant, friend, assistant, agency, subcontractor, or other third party in connection with Confidential Information without the Owner’s prior written consent.
If the Owner approves a third party, the Receiving Party shall ensure that such third party signs a confidentiality, non-use, and intellectual property assignment agreement at least as protective as this Agreement before receiving any access.
The Receiving Party remains fully responsible for any breach by any person or entity that receives Confidential Information through the Receiving Party.
12. No Portfolio, Resume, Case Study, or Publicity Use
The Receiving Party shall not use or reference the Owner, the Owner’s business, projects, apps, websites, screenshots, designs, code, product names, features, ideas, prototypes, work relationship, or Confidential Information in any portfolio, resume, LinkedIn profile, website, social media post, case study, proposal, pitch deck, advertisement, testimonial, GitHub profile, public sample, private sample, client presentation, job application, freelance-platform profile, or marketing material without the Owner’s prior written consent.
This restriction applies for ten years from the last disclosure of Confidential Information. For trade secrets, unreleased products, source code, private technical systems, customer information, financial information, and non-public business strategy, the restriction applies for as long as the information remains non-public or protected by law.
13. Ownership of Confidential Information
All Confidential Information remains the sole and exclusive property of the Owner.
Nothing in this Agreement grants the Receiving Party any ownership interest, license, equity, partnership interest, royalty right, revenue share, lien, claim, title, permission, or right to use Confidential Information except for the limited authorized purpose stated in Exhibit C or otherwise approved in writing by the Owner.
14. Work Product
“Work Product” means any work, material, code, design, document, file, improvement, invention, feature, workflow, automation, AI output, prompt, database, app component, website component, software component, business material, marketing material, product idea, technical solution, creative asset, or other deliverable that the Receiving Party creates, edits, modifies, improves, tests, contributes to, suggests, develops, or helps create:
a. for the Owner;
b. at the request of the Owner;
c. during work for the Owner;
d. using the Owner’s Confidential Information;
e. using the Owner’s accounts, tools, systems, devices, funds, facilities, credentials, files, databases, or resources;
f. based on, derived from, or related to the Owner’s apps, websites, software, businesses, workflows, systems, products, or planned products disclosed to the Receiving Party;
g. in connection with any project, assignment, task, conversation, demonstration, test, opportunity, or relationship involving the Owner.
All Work Product shall be owned exclusively by the Owner from the moment of creation.
15. Assignment of Intellectual Property Rights
The Receiving Party hereby irrevocably assigns, transfers, and conveys to the Owner all worldwide rights, title, and interest in and to all Work Product, including all copyrights, trade secret rights, patent rights to the extent assignable, trademark rights to the extent applicable, database rights, design rights, moral rights to the extent waivable, rights to sue for past, present, and future infringement, rights to register intellectual property, and rights to modify, publish, sell, license, distribute, commercialize, assign, enforce, or create derivative works from the Work Product.
The Receiving Party shall sign any documents reasonably requested by the Owner to confirm, register, enforce, or perfect the Owner’s ownership of the Work Product.
If the Receiving Party fails or refuses to sign such documents, the Receiving Party appoints the Owner as attorney-in-fact solely for the limited purpose of executing documents necessary to confirm or perfect the assignment of Work Product rights.
16. Reasonableness Carveout for Independent Inventions
This Agreement does not require assignment of an invention, work, code, design, system, product, or business idea that the Receiving Party proves through clear written records was developed entirely independently, without use of, reliance on, access to, reference to, or influence from the Owner’s Confidential Information, Work Product, trade secrets, accounts, tools, systems, devices, funds, facilities, credentials, files, databases, or resources.
This carveout does not apply to any invention, work, code, design, system, product, or business idea that:
a. was created for the Owner;
b. was requested by the Owner;
c. results from work performed for the Owner;
d. uses or is derived from Confidential Information;
e. relates to the Owner’s actual or demonstrably anticipated products, research, development, business systems, app concepts, workflows, or technical systems disclosed to the Receiving Party;
f. incorporates, reflects, or is substantially similar to Confidential Information.
17. Pre-Existing Materials
If the Receiving Party believes any pre-existing code, design, tool, template, library, framework, document, workflow, business method, invention, or intellectual property owned by the Receiving Party may be used in connection with the Owner’s project, the Receiving Party must identify it in writing in Exhibit B before use.
If the Receiving Party fails to identify such pre-existing material in Exhibit B before use, the material shall be presumed to be Work Product or, at minimum, licensed to the Owner as necessary for full use of the Work Product.
For any approved pre-existing material included in, required by, or connected to Work Product, the Receiving Party grants the Owner a perpetual, irrevocable, worldwide, royalty-free, fully paid, sublicensable, transferable license to use, reproduce, modify, distribute, display, perform, sell, commercialize, host, publish, and create derivative works from such material as part of or in connection with the Work Product.
18. Suggestions and Improvements
Any suggestion, idea, fix, improvement, feature request, design change, business idea, workflow change, technical change, code change, prompt improvement, marketing idea, product strategy, or operational recommendation suggested by the Receiving Party in connection with the Owner’s Confidential Information, Work Product, business, products, apps, websites, software, or systems shall be treated as Work Product and assigned to the Owner.
The Receiving Party shall not claim ownership, royalties, equity, credit, compensation, licensing fees, approval rights, or control based on any suggestion, improvement, or contribution unless a separate written agreement signed by the Owner expressly provides otherwise.
19. No Challenge to Ownership
The Receiving Party shall not challenge, contest, dispute, interfere with, oppose, register against, or assist any third party in challenging the Owner’s ownership of Confidential Information, Work Product, trade secrets, copyrights, trademarks, domain names, software, designs, business names, app names, product names, source code, databases, or related intellectual property.
This restriction applies for ten years from the last disclosure of Confidential Information, except as to trade secrets, Work Product, assigned intellectual property, and ownership of specific assets, for which it applies to the maximum extent permitted by law.
20. Domain Names, Handles, Business Names, and Trademarks
The Receiving Party shall not register, purchase, reserve, apply for, use, sell, transfer, or control any domain name, business name, app name, product name, trademark, social media handle, username, logo, or brand identity that is identical to, confusingly similar to, derivative of, based on, or created using the Owner’s Confidential Information, app names, business names, product names, brand names, planned names, or disclosed product concepts.
If the Receiving Party violates this section, the Receiving Party shall immediately transfer the asset to the Owner at no cost.
21. Account Access, Credentials, and Security
If the Receiving Party receives access to any account, file, repository, database, server, cloud service, admin panel, website, software, app, AI tool, domain registrar, payment processor, design file, email account, analytics account, business system, or third-party platform, the Receiving Party shall:
a. use access only for authorized purposes;
b. keep all credentials confidential;
c. not share credentials;
d. not create unauthorized users;
e. not change ownership;
f. not change billing;
g. not change recovery emails;
h. not change passwords;
i. not change permissions;
j. not export data;
k. not copy files;
l. not delete files;
m. not lock out the Owner;
n. not transfer ownership;
o. not connect unauthorized integrations;
p. not create hidden backups;
q. not create duplicate repositories;
r. not use personal accounts unless approved in writing;
s. immediately report any unauthorized access, breach, leak, loss, compromise, or suspected compromise.
All accounts, credentials, repositories, files, domains, databases, designs, systems, and workspaces created for or related to the Owner shall belong to the Owner unless a separate written agreement signed by the Owner states otherwise.
22. Non-Circumvention
The Receiving Party shall not use Confidential Information or the relationship with the Owner to bypass, avoid, interfere with, exploit, or take advantage of the Owner’s business relationships, opportunities, customers, users, vendors, contractors, developers, employees, investors, partners, venues, prospects, or leads.
The Receiving Party shall not contact, solicit, hire, contract with, sell to, provide services to, accept business from, or attempt to divert any person or entity introduced through the Owner if the purpose or effect is to compete with, avoid, replace, undercut, or harm the Owner through use of Confidential Information or through misuse of the relationship created by the Owner.
This section applies for three years from the last disclosure of Confidential Information.
23. Exclusions from Confidential Information
Confidential Information does not include information that the Receiving Party proves through clear written records:
a. was publicly known before disclosure by the Owner;
b. became publicly known through no breach, fault, or wrongful act of the Receiving Party;
c. was lawfully received from a third party without any confidentiality restriction;
d. was independently developed by the Receiving Party without use of, access to, reference to, reliance on, or influence from the Owner’s Confidential Information;
e. was expressly released in writing by the Owner.
The Receiving Party has the burden of proving that an exclusion applies.
Information shall not be excluded merely because individual parts are publicly known if the combination, structure, sequence, workflow, design, implementation, strategy, or use of those parts is not publicly known.
24. Required Legal Disclosure
If the Receiving Party is required by subpoena, court order, law, regulation, or government authority to disclose Confidential Information, the Receiving Party shall, to the extent legally permitted:
a. immediately notify the Owner in writing;
b. provide enough notice for the Owner to seek a protective order or other remedy;
c. cooperate with the Owner’s efforts to limit or prevent disclosure;
d. disclose only the minimum amount legally required;
e. continue to treat all non-disclosed information as Confidential Information.
25. Return, Deletion, and Certification
Upon the Owner’s request, or upon termination of any relationship between the Parties, the Receiving Party shall immediately:
a. return all Confidential Information;
b. permanently delete all Confidential Information from personal devices, cloud accounts, drives, emails, messages, notes, backups, repositories, AI tools, design tools, code editors, and storage locations;
c. return or transfer all accounts, repositories, files, designs, domains, code, databases, credentials, and Work Product;
d. delete all unauthorized copies, screenshots, exports, notes, recordings, summaries, and derivative materials;
e. stop using all access credentials;
f. confirm in writing that all Confidential Information has been returned, deleted, or transferred.
The Receiving Party shall not retain any copy, backup, export, screenshot, recording, summary, credential, token, API key, file, note, or derivative material unless expressly authorized in writing by the Owner.
The Receiving Party’s duty to protect Confidential Information continues after return or deletion.
26. No Waiver
The Owner’s disclosure of Confidential Information does not waive any ownership, confidentiality, trade secret, copyright, trademark, patent, contractual, or other legal rights.
The Owner’s failure to mark information confidential does not waive protection.
The Owner’s failure to object to one disclosure or breach does not waive the right to object to any other disclosure or breach.
27. No Obligation to Proceed
Nothing in this Agreement requires the Owner to hire, retain, partner with, pay, invest in, continue discussions with, or enter into any transaction with the Receiving Party.
No partnership, employment relationship, joint venture, agency relationship, franchise relationship, equity relationship, ownership relationship, or profit-sharing relationship is created by this Agreement unless a separate written agreement signed by the Owner expressly states otherwise.
28. Remedies
The Receiving Party acknowledges that breach of this Agreement may cause immediate, serious, and irreparable harm to the Owner, including loss of trade secret protection, loss of competitive advantage, loss of product value, loss of business opportunities, damage to business relationships, and damages that may be difficult to calculate.
In the event of actual or threatened breach, the Owner may seek all available remedies, including temporary restraining orders, preliminary injunctions, permanent injunctions, specific performance, return or destruction of materials, transfer of domains/accounts/repositories/files/assets, monetary damages, lost profits, disgorgement of profits, unjust enrichment damages, statutory damages where available, forensic inspection where ordered by a court, and any other remedy available under contract law, trade secret law, intellectual property law, unfair competition law, or applicable law.
The Receiving Party agrees that the Owner may seek injunctive relief without waiting for actual financial loss to occur.
29. Attorney’s Fees and Costs
In any action, arbitration, or proceeding arising out of or relating to this Agreement, the prevailing party shall be entitled to recover its reasonable attorney’s fees, court costs, arbitration costs, expert fees, forensic costs, investigation costs, and enforcement expenses to the fullest extent permitted by law.
If there is no prevailing party, each Party shall bear its own attorney’s fees and costs.
30. Indemnification
The Receiving Party shall defend, indemnify, and hold harmless the Owner from and against all claims, losses, damages, liabilities, costs, expenses, penalties, judgments, settlements, attorney’s fees, and enforcement costs arising from or related to:
a. the Receiving Party’s breach of this Agreement;
b. unauthorized disclosure of Confidential Information;
c. unauthorized use of Confidential Information;
d. misappropriation of trade secrets;
e. infringement caused by the Receiving Party’s unauthorized materials;
f. claims by third parties who received Confidential Information through the Receiving Party;
g. claims that the Receiving Party had authority to use, disclose, copy, sell, license, or commercialize the Owner’s Confidential Information or Work Product when no such authority existed.
31. Term and Survival
This Agreement begins on the Effective Date and applies to Confidential Information disclosed before, on, or after the Effective Date.
Confidentiality obligations for non-trade-secret Confidential Information continue for ten years from the date of last disclosure.
Trade secret obligations continue for as long as the information remains a trade secret under applicable law.
Ownership, Work Product, IP assignment, return/deletion, account transfer, remedies, indemnification, and payment obligations survive to the maximum extent permitted by law.
32. Governing Law and Venue
This Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of New York, without regard to conflict-of-law principles.
Any lawsuit, action, or proceeding arising from or related to this Agreement shall be brought in the state or federal courts located in New York County, New York, unless another venue is required by law.
The Receiving Party consents to personal jurisdiction and venue in such courts.
33. Severability and Reformation
If any provision of this Agreement is found invalid, illegal, or unenforceable, the remaining provisions shall remain valid and enforceable.
Any invalid, illegal, or unenforceable provision shall be modified and enforced to the maximum extent permitted by law so that it most closely reflects the original intent of the Parties.
34. Entire Agreement
This Agreement represents the entire agreement between the Parties regarding confidentiality, non-disclosure, non-use, non-circumvention, and intellectual property ownership.
Any amendment, waiver, exception, license, ownership transfer, equity grant, revenue share, partnership right, or permission must be in a written document signed by the Owner.
No verbal statement, text message, casual comment, handshake, demo, meeting, or informal communication shall modify this Agreement.
35. Electronic Signatures and Counterparts
This Agreement may be signed electronically.
A signed PDF, scanned copy, electronic signature, or digital signature has the same force and effect as an original handwritten signature.
The Parties may sign separate copies, and all signed copies together constitute one agreement.
36. Acknowledgment
The Receiving Party acknowledges that:
a. the Receiving Party has read this Agreement;
b. the Receiving Party understands this Agreement;
c. the Receiving Party had the opportunity to consult an attorney;
d. the Receiving Party is entering into this0 Agreement voluntarily;
e. violation of this Agreement may result in legal action, injunctions, damages, attorney’s fees, and other remedies;
f. the Owner is relying on this Agreement before disclosing or continuing to disclose Confidential Information.

Attorney Review Brief
Give this to a New York attorney:
Client goal: Founder wants a one-way NDA, non-use, non-circumvention, and IP assignment agreement protecting current and future apps, websites, SaaS products, AI workflows, source code, designs, business models, and service-business/hospitality software concepts.
Relationship type: Friend / potential cofounder / possible contractor. No equity should be implied unless separately documented.
State: New York.
Key legal concerns to review:
Consideration for current and prior disclosures.
Retroactive protection for information already disclosed.
Scope of “future apps” and “planned products.”
Non-use language to avoid de facto non-compete treatment.
Work Product assignment language for cofounder/contractor scenarios.
Treatment of pre-existing code, templates, libraries, and AI-generated output.
AI-tool confidentiality restrictions.
GitHub/Vercel/Supabase/Figma/Wix/Replit account ownership and access-control language.
Non-circumvention period and scope.
Attorney’s fees clause.
Whether arbitration, jury waiver, or emergency injunctive relief language should be added.
Whether a separate cofounder agreement, contractor agreement, equity agreement, or IP assignment confirmation is needed.
Documents still needed if this person becomes a real cofounder:
Cofounder agreement.
IP assignment confirmation.
Equity vesting agreement.
Operating agreement or shareholder agreement.
Confidentiality and invention assignment agreement.
Account-access checklist.
GitHub/Vercel/Supabase/Figma/domain ownership checklist.
The NDA protects secrecy. It does not by itself solve equity, founder ownership, voting rights, vesting, payment, title, firing rights, or who owns the company.Fork

Thank you for reaching out and for thinking of us for your honor roll field trip. We would be happy to accommodate your group of approximately 50 students and 5 chaperones.

For a group that size, we would reserve both of our VIP private rooms exclusively for your school. Our standard evening rate is $240 per hour per VIP room (plus tax and gratuity). We do recommend an earlier field-trip time starting the room before 5:00pm to get 2 hours at the happy hour room rate. We can offer you the happy hour room rate of $300 per hour total for both VIP rooms.

At that rate, the pricing works out to approximately:

$300 per hour for both VIP rooms

$383.63 PER hour including 8.875% tax and 19% gratuity

$394.13 PER hour if paid by credit/debit card, which includes a 3.5% card processing fee

Please let us know your preferred date in early February, the start time, and how many hours you would like to reserve, and we can draft a detailed estimate and hold the rooms for your group.

Thank you again, and we look forward to the possibility of hosting your students.

Best regards,

I am writing to introduce Christmas Karaoke, a private-room venue in Bayside, Queens, as a potential option for your organization’s corporate holiday party or year-end corporate event.

Our venue provides fully private rooms designed to accommodate corporate groups in a contained and controlled environment. We are frequently selected by Long Island–based organizations due to the following considerations:

- Bring-your-own food and beverages, allowing organizations full flexibility in catering selection and budget management
- Convenient access from Nassau County and western Suffolk, avoiding the travel burden and congestion associated with Manhattan venues
- Ample nearby parking, simplifying logistics for attendees
- Private, enclosed rooms, ensuring a professional and non-crowded setting suitable for corporate gatherings

Cost-control justification:
The ability to bring outside food and beverages allows organizations to manage overall event costs more efficiently compared to venues with mandatory in-house catering or minimum spend requirements.

If helpful, I would be pleased to provide:

- Room configurations based on anticipated attendance
- December availability options
- A clear and itemized cost overview suitable for internal approval

Please feel free to contact me should you wish to explore this option further or request additional information.

Kind regards,

This is your most Instagram-friendly workflow:
🎥 Why It Works
✅ Vertical format (9:16)
✅ Clean compositing (characters over your real store video)
✅ Looks intentionally comedic (not creepy uncanny valley)
✅ No huge costs
🧠 How It Works
Film your store video with your phone (vertical).
Generate Bigfoot character clips from Pika Labs.
Composite them into your store footage using CapCut + masking/tracking.
No need for expensive credits, and reels will look shareable and fun.
🥈 Runway (Fast & More Realistic if You Want Slightly Better Quality)
Still good for Instagram, but: 👉 Costs credits
👉 Takes more time to render
👉 Results can be very cinematic
Use Runway if you want:
✔ More polished look
✔ Better lighting match
✔ Motion tracking without manual masking
If you don’t want to pay: go with CapCut + Pika Labs.
📌 Step-By-Step: Instagram Version (CapCut + Pika Labs)
🎬 STEP 1 — Film Your Store Video
📱 Vertical (9:16)
🔹 8–12 seconds
🕹 Slow or static camera
💡 Keep lighting consistent
This footage becomes your background.
🤖 STEP 2 — Generate Bigfoot + Sasquatch Clips (Free/Semi-Free)
Use Pika Labs (has some free credits for new users):
Prompt for Pika:
Cartoonish drunken Bigfoot + white Sasquatch sneaking beers, comedic exaggerated movements, neon lighting, playful vibe, loopable animated clip, transparent background if possible.
Export as: 🎞️ Short loop (5–10 sec) 🟩 Preferably with alpha (transparent) if Pika gives it
🎨 STEP 3 — Composite in CapCut
Import your store video
Set as base layer
Import Bigfoot/Sasquatch animations
Place on top
Use Cutout / Masking / Layering
Scale characters appropriately
Slide them walking into frame
Add motion tracking
If they need to follow a lateral path
Add sound + text captions
Punchlines appear fast (Instagram tempo)
🎧 Suggested Audio Elements
✔ Clumsy footsteps
✔ Slurred comical voices
✔ Record scratch effect when employee appears
✔ Funny boing / slide whistle cues
✔ Text overlay dialogue bubbles
🎯 Insta Optimization Tips
📍 First 2-3 seconds must hook.
E.g.: “Wait… WHAT are these guys doing? 😭”
📍 Use bold captions / emojis
People watch reels WITHOUT SOUND first.
📍 Keep it 10–20 seconds
Short = more replays + algorithm boost.
📍 Add trending audio if it fits
Or use punchy SFX.
📍 Text on screen must be fast + funny
Bold white outline — easy to read.
⚡ Example On-Screen Text Flow
0–2s: “When Bigfoot & Sasquatch sneak BEER into karaoke…”
3–5s: Whisper yelling “Act natural!”
6–8s: Employee pops in 😳
9–11s: Employee: “Chill… BYOB is allowed.”
12–15s: Bigfoot: “You could’ve said that!”
15–18s: They’re already singing off-key 🎤😂
🥳 Why This Will Work on Instagram
✨ Fast edit
✨ Comedic characters
✨ Relatable scenario (karaoke vibes!)
✨ Shareable & meme-able
